--==================================================
-- AIM MODE OVERRIDES
--==================================================

-- Rage does not copy the Aim tab's values and overwrite them; it substitutes
-- its own at read time. Switching Rage off therefore restores whatever the
-- player had configured, with no save/restore bookkeeping to get wrong.
local function AimCfg(key)
    if Config.RageEnabled then
        local value = Config["Rage" .. key]
        if value ~= nil then
            return value
        end
    end
    return Config["Aim" .. key]
end

-- Rage can run by itself: turning the toggle on is enough, no separate Aimbot
-- master switch. RageRequireKey puts the aim key back in charge.
local function AimActive()
    return Config.AimEnabled or Config.RageEnabled
end

--==================================================
-- RUNTIME STATE
--==================================================

local State = {
    -- aim
    Target = nil,
    TargetPart = nil,
    AimHeld = false,
    AimToggled = false,
    AimFiring = false,
    LastAimKey = 0,
    AimGain = 0,
    AimLastDistance = nil,
    AimLastPlayer = nil,
    AimMoveOK = nil,
    AimStatus = "idle",
    AimPoint = nil,                 -- per-frame predicted/offset aim point, shared by both paths
    AimPointAt = 0,
    AimPointFrame = nil,            -- render frame the point was built on, so one frame reuses one point
    AimDelivery = "mouse",          -- "mouse" | "camera", what is actually moving the view
    AimStall = 0,                   -- frames the pointer pushed counts and the error did not drop
    AimRetryAt = 0,                 -- when a stalled pointer gets another chance
    AimLostFrames = 0,              -- frames a camera write did not survive to the next frame

    -- teams: Rivals ships no Team objects, only a TeamID attribute, and in a
    -- free-for-all that attribute can be identical for everyone. TeamMode
    -- records whether the server actually has more than one side.
    TeamMode = false,
    TeamCount = 0,

    -- trigger
    TriggerHeld = false,
    LastTrigger = 0,

    -- caches
    Cache = {},
    CacheAge = 0,
    Drawings = {},
    Highlights = {},
    Bodies = {},
    Expanded = {},
    Hitboxes = {},

    -- local
    FlyVelocity = Vector3.zero,
    FlyAttachment = nil,
    Spectating = nil,
    PreviousCamera = nil,
    Backup = {},
    StartedAt = os.clock(),
    FrameTime = 0,
    FPS = 60,
    FrameCount = 0,
    LastFPS = os.clock(),

    -- hud
    HitSounds = {},
    MarkerUntil = 0,
    DamageFeed = {},
    DamageNumbers = {},
    HealthTrack = {},
    LastFire = 0,
    Coordinates = "",
    Notifications = {},
}

--==================================================
-- SMALL UTILITIES
--==================================================

local Util = {}

function Util.Clamp(value, low, high)
    if value < low then
        return low
    elseif value > high then
        return high
    end
    return value
end

function Util.Lerp(a, b, t)
    return a + (b - a) * t
end

function Util.Round(value, step)
    step = step or 1
    return math.floor(value / step + 0.5) * step
end

function Util.Distance(a, b)
    return (a - b).Magnitude
end

function Util.Flat(pos)
    return Vector3.new(pos.X, 0, pos.Z)
end

function Util.Safe(fn, ...)
    local ok, result = pcall(fn, ...)
    if ok then
        return result
    end
    return nil
end

function Util.SafeCall(fn, ...)
    if type(fn) ~= "function" then
        return
    end
    local args = table.pack(...)
    local ok, err = pcall(function()
        fn(table.unpack(args, 1, args.n))
    end)
    if not ok then
        warn("[RIVALS] callback error: " .. tostring(err))
    end
end

function Util.SerializeColor(color)
    return {
        R = math.floor(color.R * 255 + 0.5),
        G = math.floor(color.G * 255 + 0.5),
        B = math.floor(color.B * 255 + 0.5),
    }
end

function Util.DeserializeColor(value)
    if typeof(value) == "Color3" then
        return value
    end
    if type(value) == "table" then
        return Color3.fromRGB(
            Util.Clamp(tonumber(value.R) or 255, 0, 255),
            Util.Clamp(tonumber(value.G) or 255, 0, 255),
            Util.Clamp(tonumber(value.B) or 255, 0, 255)
        )
    end
    return Color3.fromRGB(255, 255, 255)
end

function Util.FormatNumber(value)
    if value >= 1e9 then
        return string.format("%.1fB", value / 1e9)
    elseif value >= 1e6 then
        return string.format("%.1fM", value / 1e6)
    elseif value >= 1e3 then
        return string.format("%.1fK", value / 1e3)
    end
    return tostring(math.floor(value))
end

function Util.Round2(value)
    return math.floor(value * 100 + 0.5) / 100
end

function Util.Readable(value)
    local text = tostring(value)
    text = text:gsub("(%l)(%u)", "%1 %2")
    text = text:gsub("_", " ")
    return text
end

function Util.GetExecutor()
    if identifyexecutor then
        local ok, name = pcall(identifyexecutor)
        if ok and type(name) == "string" then
            return name
        end
    end
    return "Unknown"
end

--==================================================
-- INPUT PRIMITIVES
--==================================================

-- Rivals runs Enum.CameraType.Scriptable and writes the camera itself every
-- frame, so a RenderStepped CFrame write is overwritten before it renders.
-- Steering the pointer instead feeds the same delta the game's own camera
-- code reads, which survives the game taking the camera back. Everything
-- below is feature-detected because not every executor exposes native input.

function Util.HasMouseMove()
    return type(mousemoverel) == "function"
end

function Util.HasMouseClick()
    return type(mouse1click) == "function"
end

function Util.Active()
    if type(isrbxactive) == "function" then
        local ok, value = pcall(isrbxactive)
        if ok then
            return value == true
        end
    end
    return true
end

-- Rivals only routes synthetic pointer deltas into the camera while it is
-- holding the cursor. With MouseBehavior.Default the same counts drag the OS
-- pointer across the desktop and the view does not move at all, which is the
-- single most common reason pointer aim reads as broken.
function Util.PointerCaptured()
    local behavior = UserInputService.MouseBehavior
    return behavior == Enum.MouseBehavior.LockCenter
        or behavior == Enum.MouseBehavior.LockCurrentPosition
end

-- Counts, not pixels: the game scales the delta by its own sensitivity, so the
-- caller works in screen pixels and this is the throttle that stops one huge
-- frame from throwing the view across the map.
function Util.MoveMouse(dx, dy)
    if type(mousemoverel) ~= "function" then
        return false
    end
    local x = math.floor(dx + (dx >= 0 and 0.5 or -0.5))
    local y = math.floor(dy + (dy >= 0 and 0.5 or -0.5))
    if x == 0 and y == 0 then
        return true
    end
    local ok = pcall(mousemoverel, x, y)
    return ok
end

function Util.Click()
    if type(mouse1click) == "function" then
        local ok = pcall(mouse1click)
        if ok then
            return true
        end
    end
    local ok = pcall(function()
        local virtual = VirtualUser or game:GetService("VirtualUser")
        virtual:CaptureController()
        virtual:ClickButton1(Vector2.new())
    end)
    return ok
end

-- Live key state rather than the InputBegan event: a game that consumes a
-- keypress (right mouse for ADS, for example) still leaves the physical key
-- down, and the event path would never see it.
function Util.KeyHeld(name)
    if type(name) ~= "string" or name == "" then
        return false
    end

    if string.find(name, "MouseButton", 1, true) then
        local ok, inputType = pcall(function()
            return Enum.UserInputType[name]
        end)
        if ok and inputType then
            return UserInputService:IsMouseButtonPressed(inputType)
        end
        return false
    end

    local ok, keyCode = pcall(function()
        return Enum.KeyCode[name]
    end)
    if ok and keyCode then
        return UserInputService:IsKeyDown(keyCode)
    end
    return false
end


--==================================================
-- PLAYER HELPERS
--==================================================

local PlayerUtil = {}

function PlayerUtil.GetCharacter(player)
    if not player then
        return nil
    end
    local character = player.Character
    if not character or not character.Parent then
        return nil
    end
    return character
end

function PlayerUtil.GetHumanoid(player)
    local character = PlayerUtil.GetCharacter(player)
    if not character then
        return nil
    end
    local humanoid = character:FindFirstChildOfClass("Humanoid")
    if humanoid and humanoid.Health > 0 then
        return humanoid
    end
    return humanoid
end

function PlayerUtil.GetRoot(player)
    local character = PlayerUtil.GetCharacter(player)
    if not character then
        return nil
    end
    return character:FindFirstChild("HumanoidRootPart")
end

function PlayerUtil.IsAlive(player)
    local humanoid = PlayerUtil.GetHumanoid(player)
    if not humanoid then
        return false
    end
    return humanoid.Health > 0
end

-- Rivals sets no Team objects at all: Players:GetChildren() holds no Team and
-- Player.Team is nil for every player in the server. Side information rides on
-- the player as a numeric TeamID attribute instead, and is simply absent in a
-- free-for-all lobby. Read the attribute first so the team filter still does
-- something in a duel or a team mode, then fall back to Team for any place that
-- ever does populate one.
function PlayerUtil.GetTeamId(player)
    if not player then
        return nil
    end
    -- Rivals does not use Team objects. The side lives in a TeamID attribute
    -- holding a one-character control byte ("\1", "\2"), which is a string, not
    -- a number - a type(attribute) == "number" check rejected every real value,
    -- so every player read as an enemy and the team filter never did anything.
    local attribute = player:GetAttribute("TeamID")
    if attribute ~= nil then
        local kind = typeof(attribute)
        if kind == "EnumItem" then
            return "e:" .. attribute.Name
        end
        return "a:" .. tostring(attribute)
    end
    local team = player.Team
    if team then
        return "t:" .. tostring(team.TeamId or team.Name)
    end
    return nil
end

function PlayerUtil.IsTeammate(player)
    if not player or player == LocalPlayer then
        return false
    end
    -- Free-for-all guard: one distinct TeamID across the server means the
    -- attribute is not describing sides, and filtering on it would drop every
    -- player in the game - which reads as "the aimbot does nothing".
    if not State.TeamMode then
        return false
    end
    local mine = PlayerUtil.GetTeamId(LocalPlayer)
    local theirs = PlayerUtil.GetTeamId(player)
    if mine == nil or theirs == nil then
        return false
    end
    return mine == theirs
end

function PlayerUtil.IsFriend(player)
    if not player or player == LocalPlayer then
        return false
    end
    local ok, result = pcall(function()
        return LocalPlayer:IsFriendsWith(player.UserId)
    end)
    return ok and result == true
end

-- A fighter in the hub carries no Tool and an empty Backpack, so the Tool path
-- is only ever live mid-match. Check the attributes Rivals mirrors loadout state
-- onto the player and the character next, and give up cleanly if neither has
-- anything - an unknown weapon draws nothing rather than a wrong name.
function PlayerUtil.GetWeapon(player)
    local character = PlayerUtil.GetCharacter(player)
    if not character then
        return nil
    end
    local tool = character:FindFirstChildOfClass("Tool")
    if tool then
        return tool.Name
    end
    local attributes = {
        "Weapon",
        "CurrentWeapon",
        "EquippedWeapon",
        "Gun",
        "Item",
        "WeaponName",
    }
    for _, source in ipairs({ player, character }) do
        for _, name in ipairs(attributes) do
            local value = source:GetAttribute(name)
            if type(value) == "string" and value ~= "" then
                return value
            end
        end
    end
    return nil
end

function PlayerUtil.GetDisplayName(player)
    local ok, name = pcall(function()
        return player.DisplayName
    end)
    if ok and type(name) == "string" and name ~= "" then
        return name
    end
    return player.Name
end

function PlayerUtil.GetPart(player, partName)
    local character = PlayerUtil.GetCharacter(player)
    if not character then
        return nil
    end

    if partName == "Nearest" then
        local root = character:FindFirstChild("HumanoidRootPart")
        if not root then
            return nil
        end
        local best, bestDistance = nil, math.huge
        for _, part in ipairs(character:GetDescendants()) do
            if part:IsA("BasePart") and part.Name ~= "HumanoidRootPart" then
                local distance = (part.Position - root.Position).Magnitude
                if distance < bestDistance then
                    bestDistance = distance
                    best = part
                end
            end
        end
        return best or root
    end

    -- Rivals builds its own hitbox rig on every fighter instead of relying on
    -- the limb parts alone. Verified live on R15 characters:
    --   HitboxHead       2 x 2 x 2        IsCritical = true
    --   HitboxBody       3.5 x 4 x 2.5
    --   HitboxHeadSmall  1.75^3           IsSmallHitbox = true
    --   HitboxBodySmall  2.75 x 4 x 1.75  IsSmallHitbox = true
    --   PhysicalHitboxHead                the only one with CanCollide = true
    -- All five are Transparency 1 and Massless. Aiming at these aims at the boxes
    -- the game resolves damage against, which is worth more than aiming at the
    -- cosmetic MeshPart that happens to sit in the same place.
    if Config.AimEntityHitboxes then
        local entity = {
            Head = { "HitboxHead", "HitboxHeadSmall", "PhysicalHitboxHead" },
            Neck = { "HitboxBody", "HitboxBodySmall" },
            Torso = { "HitboxBody", "HitboxBodySmall" },
            Chest = { "HitboxBody", "HitboxBodySmall" },
            Pelvis = { "HitboxBody", "HitboxBodySmall" },
        }
        for _, name in ipairs(entity[partName] or {}) do
            local hitbox = character:FindFirstChild(name)
            if hitbox and hitbox:IsA("BasePart") then
                return hitbox
            end
        end
    end

    local names = {
        Head = "Head",
        Neck = "UpperTorso",
        Torso = "UpperTorso",
        Chest = "UpperTorso",
        Pelvis = "LowerTorso",
        Root = "HumanoidRootPart",
        Legs = "RightUpperLeg",
    }

    local primary = names[partName] or partName
    local part = character:FindFirstChild(primary)
    if part and part:IsA("BasePart") then
        return part
    end

    local fallbacks = {
        UpperTorso = { "Torso", "HumanoidRootPart" },
        LowerTorso = { "Torso", "HumanoidRootPart" },
        Head = { "HumanoidRootPart" },
        RightUpperLeg = { "RightLeg", "RightUpperLeg", "HumanoidRootPart" },
    }

    for _, name in ipairs(fallbacks[primary] or {}) do
        local fallback = character:FindFirstChild(name)
        if fallback and fallback:IsA("BasePart") then
            return fallback
        end
    end

    return character:FindFirstChild("HumanoidRootPart")
end

function PlayerUtil.GetHealth(player)
    local humanoid = PlayerUtil.GetHumanoid(player)
    if not humanoid then
        return 0, 100
    end
    return humanoid.Health, humanoid.MaxHealth
end

function PlayerUtil.IsVisible(player)
    local character = PlayerUtil.GetCharacter(player)
    local localCharacter = PlayerUtil.GetCharacter(LocalPlayer)
    if not character or not localCharacter then
        return false
    end

    local part = PlayerUtil.GetPart(player, Config.AimPart)
    local origin = PlayerUtil.GetPart(LocalPlayer, "Head")
    if not part or not origin then
        return false
    end

    local params = RaycastParams.new()
    params.FilterType = Enum.RaycastFilterType.Exclude
    params.FilterDescendantsInstances = { localCharacter, character }
    params.IgnoreWater = true

    local result = Workspace:Raycast(origin.Position, part.Position - origin.Position, params)
    return result == nil
end

--==================================================
-- DRAWING LIBRARY WRAPPER
--==================================================

local Draw = {}

Draw.Available = false
Draw.Objects = {}

pcall(function()
    if Drawing ~= nil then
        Draw.Available = true
    end
end)

function Draw.New(kind)
    if not Draw.Available then
        return nil
    end

    local ok, object = pcall(function()
        return Drawing.new(kind)
    end)

    if not ok or not object then
        return nil
    end

    object.Visible = false
    table.insert(Draw.Objects, object)
    return object
end

function Draw.Remove(object)
    if not object then
        return
    end
    pcall(function()
        object:Remove()
    end)
end

function Draw.Set(object, properties)
    if not object then
        return
    end
    for key, value in pairs(properties) do
        pcall(function()
            object[key] = value
        end)
    end
end

function Draw.Show(object, visible)
    if not object then
        return
    end
    pcall(function()
        object.Visible = visible
    end)
end

function Draw.ClearAll()
    for _, object in ipairs(Draw.Objects) do
        pcall(function()
            object:Remove()
        end)
    end
    table.clear(Draw.Objects)
    table.clear(State.Drawings)
end

--==================================================
-- DRAWING POOL
--==================================================

local Pool = {}

function Pool.Get(key, kind)
    local object = State.Drawings[key]
    if object then
        return object
    end
    object = Draw.New(kind)
    State.Drawings[key] = object
    return object
end

function Pool.Release(keyPrefix)
    -- Collected first: the loop clears the key it is standing on, and a pairs
    -- loop whose key is deleted skips the entry that fills the freed slot.
    local drop = {}
    for key in pairs(State.Drawings) do
        if string.sub(key, 1, #keyPrefix) == keyPrefix then
            drop[key] = true
        end
    end
    for key in pairs(drop) do
        Draw.Remove(State.Drawings[key])
        State.Drawings[key] = nil
    end
end

--==================================================
-- CAMERA HELPERS
--==================================================

local Cam = {}

-- Workspace.CurrentCamera is replaced by some games on respawn or when a
-- camera script swaps the view. Holding the old instance means every
-- WorldToScreen call projects through a camera nobody renders with, which
-- looks exactly like "the ESP is broken".
function Cam.Get()
    local current = Workspace.CurrentCamera
    if current and current.Parent then
        Camera = current
    end
    return Camera
end

function Cam.Position()
    local camera = Cam.Get()
    if not camera then
        return Vector3.zero
    end
    return camera.CFrame.Position
end

function Cam.Angles()
    local camera = Cam.Get()
    if not camera then
        return Vector2.zero
    end
    local look = camera.CFrame.LookVector
    local yaw = math.atan2(-look.X, -look.Z)
    local pitch = math.asin(Util.Clamp(look.Y, -1, 1))
    return Vector2.new(math.deg(yaw), math.deg(pitch))
end

function Cam.WorldToScreen(position)
    local camera = Cam.Get()
    if not camera then
        return nil, false, 0
    end
    local point, onScreen = camera:WorldToViewportPoint(position)
    if point.Z <= 0 then
        return Vector2.new(point.X, point.Y), false, point.Z
    end
    return Vector2.new(point.X, point.Y), onScreen, point.Z
end

function Cam.ViewportSize()
    local camera = Cam.Get()
    if not camera then
        return Vector2.new(1920, 1080)
    end
    return camera.ViewportSize
end

function Cam.ScreenCenter()
    local size = Cam.ViewportSize()
    return Vector2.new(size.X / 2, size.Y / 2)
end

-- Distance from the crosshair to a screen point, in pixels.
function Cam.CrosshairDistance(screenPoint)
    return (screenPoint - Cam.ScreenCenter()).Magnitude
end

-- FOV is a radius in pixels. 0 would mean "always snap", so it is clamped
-- to a sane floor at the call site rather than here.
function Cam.InFOV(screenPoint, fov)
    if fov <= 0 then
        return true
    end
    return Cam.CrosshairDistance(screenPoint) <= fov
end

--==================================================
-- PLAYER CACHE
--==================================================

local Cache = {}

Cache.List = {}
Cache.LastBuild = 0
Cache.Interval = 0.15

local function BuildCache()
    local entries = {}
    local localCharacter = PlayerUtil.GetCharacter(LocalPlayer)
    local myPosition = localCharacter and localCharacter:FindFirstChild("HumanoidRootPart")

    myPosition = myPosition and myPosition.Position or Cam.Position()

    -- Count distinct sides before deciding whether a team filter means
    -- anything. Rivals has no Team objects; the only signal is the TeamID
    -- attribute, and a free-for-all server can still carry one shared value.
    local sides = {}
    local sideCount = 0
    for _, player in ipairs(Players:GetPlayers()) do
        local id = PlayerUtil.GetTeamId(player)
        if id ~= nil then
            local key = tostring(id)
            if sides[key] == nil then
                sides[key] = true
                sideCount = sideCount + 1
            end
        end
    end

    State.TeamMode = sideCount >= 2
    State.TeamCount = sideCount

    for _, player in ipairs(Players:GetPlayers()) do
        if player ~= LocalPlayer then
            local character = PlayerUtil.GetCharacter(player)
            if character then
                local humanoid = character:FindFirstChildOfClass("Humanoid")
                local root = character:FindFirstChild("HumanoidRootPart")

                if humanoid and root then
                    local alive = humanoid.Health > 0
                    local teammate = PlayerUtil.IsTeammate(player)
                    local distance = (root.Position - myPosition).Magnitude

                    table.insert(entries, {
                        Player = player,
                        Character = character,
                        Humanoid = humanoid,
                        Root = root,
                        Alive = alive,
                        Teammate = teammate,
                        Distance = distance,
                        Health = humanoid.Health,
                        MaxHealth = humanoid.MaxHealth,
                        Name = player.Name,
                        DisplayName = PlayerUtil.GetDisplayName(player),
                        Weapon = PlayerUtil.GetWeapon(player),
                        -- Rivals mirrors progression onto the player itself.
                        -- Level and DisplayELO are numbers, PlayerStatus is the
                        -- mode flag ("Contraband") and is absent most of the time.
                        Level = player:GetAttribute("Level"),
                        ELO = player:GetAttribute("DisplayELO"),
                        Status = player:GetAttribute("PlayerStatus"),
                    })
                end
            end
        end
    end

    Cache.List = entries
    Cache.LastBuild = os.clock()
end

function Cache.Get()
    if os.clock() - Cache.LastBuild >= Cache.Interval then
        BuildCache()
    end
    return Cache.List
end

function Cache.Force()
    BuildCache()
end

function Cache.Find(player)
    for _, entry in ipairs(Cache.Get()) do
        if entry.Player == player then
            return entry
        end
    end
    return nil
end

function Cache.Clear()
    Cache.List = {}
end

-- A target is only worth locking if it survived the filter set the user
-- asked for. Every check is a hard `return false`, never a warning.
function Cache.IsTargetable(entry, options)
    if not entry then
        return false
    end

    options = options or {}

    if options.IgnoreDead ~= false and not entry.Alive then
        return false
    end

    if options.TeamCheck and entry.Teammate then
        return false
    end

    if options.IgnoreFriends and PlayerUtil.IsFriend(entry.Player) then
        return false
    end

    if options.MaxDistance and entry.Distance > options.MaxDistance then
        return false
    end

    return true
end

--==================================================
-- ASTER UI
--==================================================

local loaded, Aster = pcall(function()
    return loadstring(game:HttpGet(
        "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/podivichdev/Aster-UI/main/Library.lua"
    ))()
end)

if not loaded or not Aster then
    warn("[RIVALS] Aster UI failed to load: " .. tostring(Aster))
    return
end

    Title = "RIVALS // SIGNAL",
    Subtitle = "TACTICAL CONTROL INTERFACE",
    LoadingTitle = "RIVALS 1.4.0",
    LoadingSubtitle = "INITIALIZING SYSTEMS",
    ToggleUIKeybind = "RightShift",
    Icon = "None",
    Theme = Config.Theme,
    Weather = "Snow",
    Backdrop = { Dim = true },
    AlwaysOnTop = true,
    AutoTranslate = false,
    ShowCustomCursor = false,
    ConfigurationSaving = {
        FolderName = "RIVALS",
        FileName = "RIVALS_1.0.0",
    },
    Home = {
        Stats = {
            "FPS",
            "Ping",
            "Game",
            "Players",
            "ServerAge",
            "Session",
            "Executor",
        },
    },
})

_G.RIVALS_WINDOW = Window
_G.RIVALS_CONFIG = Config
_G.RIVALS_ASTER = Aster
_G.RIVALS_STATE = State
-- Debug handle. Util.Click is the one call that leaves the process, so a live
-- probe needs a way to stub it and count trigger fires without clicking.
_G.RIVALS_UTIL = Util

local function Notify(title, message, icon, duration)
    if not Config.Notifications then
        return
    end
    pcall(function()
        Window:Notify({
            Title = title,
            Content = message,
            Icon = icon or "info",
            Duration = duration or 3,
        })
    end)
end

local function UiContainer()
    if gethui then
        local ok, parent = pcall(gethui)
        if ok and parent then
            return parent
        end
    end
    return LocalPlayer:WaitForChild("PlayerGui")
end

local ScreenGui = Instance.new("ScreenGui")
ScreenGui.Name = "RIVALS_Overlay"
ScreenGui.ResetOnSpawn = false
ScreenGui.IgnoreGuiInset = true
ScreenGui.DisplayOrder = 40
ScreenGui.Parent = UiContainer()

_G.RIVALS_GUI = ScreenGui

--==================================================
-- NOTIFICATION STACK

local Toast = {}
Toast.Container = nil
Toast.Items = {}

local function EnsureToastContainer()
    if Toast.Container and Toast.Container.Parent then
        return Toast.Container
    end

    local frame = Instance.new("Frame")
    frame.Name = "Toasts"
    frame.AnchorPoint = Vector2.new(1, 0)
    frame.Position = UDim2.new(1, -24, 0, 220)
    frame.Size = UDim2.fromOffset(280, 400)
    frame.BackgroundTransparency = 1
    frame.Parent = ScreenGui

    local layout = Instance.new("UIListLayout")
    layout.Padding = UDim.new(0, 6)
    layout.SortOrder = Enum.SortOrder.LayoutOrder
    layout.Parent = frame

    Toast.Container = frame
    return frame
end

function Toast.Push(title, text, color)
    if not Config.Notifications then
        return
    end

    local container = EnsureToastContainer()

    local card = Instance.new("Frame")
    card.Size = UDim2.new(1, 0, 0, 46)
    card.BackgroundColor3 = Color3.fromRGB(16, 16, 22)
    card.BackgroundTransparency = 0.1
    card.BorderSizePixel = 0
    card.Parent = container

    local corner = Instance.new("UICorner")
    corner.CornerRadius = UDim.new(0, 8)
    corner.Parent = card

    local stroke = Instance.new("UIStroke")
    stroke.Color = color or Config.AccentColor
    stroke.Transparency = 0.4
    stroke.Parent = card

    local bar = Instance.new("Frame")
    bar.Size = UDim2.new(0, 3, 1, -12)
    bar.Position = UDim2.new(0, 6, 0, 6)
    bar.BackgroundColor3 = color or Config.AccentColor
    bar.BorderSizePixel = 0
    bar.Parent = card

    local barCorner = Instance.new("UICorner")
    barCorner.CornerRadius = UDim.new(1, 0)
    barCorner.Parent = bar

    local titleLabel = Instance.new("TextLabel")
    titleLabel.Position = UDim2.new(0, 16, 0, 6)
    titleLabel.Size = UDim2.new(1, -24, 0, 16)
    titleLabel.BackgroundTransparency = 1
    titleLabel.Font = Enum.Font.GothamBold
    titleLabel.TextSize = 12
    titleLabel.TextColor3 = Color3.fromRGB(240, 240, 245)
    titleLabel.TextXAlignment = Enum.TextXAlignment.Left
    titleLabel.Text = title
    titleLabel.Parent = card

    local bodyLabel = Instance.new("TextLabel")
    bodyLabel.Position = UDim2.new(0, 16, 0, 23)
    bodyLabel.Size = UDim2.new(1, -24, 0, 16)
    bodyLabel.BackgroundTransparency = 1
    bodyLabel.Font = Enum.Font.Gotham
    bodyLabel.TextSize = 11
    bodyLabel.TextColor3 = Color3.fromRGB(150, 150, 165)
    bodyLabel.TextXAlignment = Enum.TextXAlignment.Left
    bodyLabel.Text = text
    bodyLabel.Parent = card

    table.insert(Toast.Items, card)

    task.delay(3, function()
        if not card or not card.Parent then
            return
        end
        local info = TweenInfo.new(0.25, Enum.EasingStyle.Quad, Enum.EasingDirection.In)
        TweenService:Create(card, info, {
            BackgroundTransparency = 1,
        }):Play()
        TweenService:Create(titleLabel, info, { TextTransparency = 1 }):Play()
        TweenService:Create(bodyLabel, info, { TextTransparency = 1 }):Play()
        task.wait(0.28)
        card:Destroy()
    end)
end

--==================================================
-- BACKUPS FOR LOCAL RESTORES
--==================================================

local Backup = State.Backup

local function CaptureBackup()
    Backup.ClockTime = Lighting.ClockTime
    Backup.Brightness = Lighting.Brightness
    Backup.OutdoorAmbient = Lighting.OutdoorAmbient
    Backup.Ambient = Lighting.Ambient
    Backup.FogEnd = Lighting.FogEnd
    Backup.FogStart = Lighting.FogStart
    Backup.FogColor = Lighting.FogColor
    Backup.ExposureCompensation = Lighting.ExposureCompensation
    Backup.CameraFOV = Camera and Camera.FieldOfView or 70
    Backup.Sky = Lighting:FindFirstChildOfClass("Sky")
    Backup.HadSky = Backup.Sky ~= nil
end

CaptureBackup()

local function RestoreBackup()
    pcall(function()
        if Backup.ClockTime then
            Lighting.ClockTime = Backup.ClockTime
        end
        if Backup.Brightness then
            Lighting.Brightness = Backup.Brightness
        end
        if Backup.OutdoorAmbient then
            Lighting.OutdoorAmbient = Backup.OutdoorAmbient
        end
        if Backup.Ambient then
            Lighting.Ambient = Backup.Ambient
        end
        if Backup.FogEnd then
            Lighting.FogEnd = Backup.FogEnd
        end
        if Backup.FogStart then
            Lighting.FogStart = Backup.FogStart
        end
        if Backup.FogColor then
            Lighting.FogColor = Backup.FogColor
        end
        if Backup.CameraFOV and Camera then
            Camera.FieldOfView = Backup.CameraFOV
        end
    end)
end

--==================================================
-- LIGHTING EFFECTS
--==================================================

local Effects = {}

Effects.Tint = nil
Effects.SkyBackup = nil

function Effects.EnsureTint()
    if Effects.Tint and Effects.Tint.Parent then
        return Effects.Tint
    end
    local effect = Instance.new("ColorCorrectionEffect")
    effect.Name = "RIVALS_Tint"
    effect.Parent = Lighting
    Effects.Tint = effect
    return effect
end

function Effects.ApplyTint()
    if not Config.WorldTint then
        if Effects.Tint then
            Effects.Tint.Enabled = false
        end
        return
    end

    local effect = Effects.EnsureTint()
    effect.Enabled = true
    effect.TintColor = Config.WorldTintColor
    effect.Brightness = Config.WorldTintBrightness
    effect.Contrast = Config.WorldTintContrast
    effect.Saturation = Config.WorldTintSaturation
end

function Effects.ApplyFog()
    if Config.RemoveFog then
        Lighting.FogStart = 0
        Lighting.FogEnd = 100000
    else
        Lighting.FogStart = Backup.FogStart or 0
        Lighting.FogEnd = Backup.FogEnd or 100000
    end
end

function Effects.ApplySkybox()
    if Config.NoSkybox then
        local sky = Lighting:FindFirstChildOfClass("Sky")
        if sky then
            if not Effects.SkyBackup then
                Effects.SkyBackup = {}
                Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxBk = sky.SkyboxBk
                Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxDn = sky.SkyboxDn
                Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxFt = sky.SkyboxFt
                Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxLf = sky.SkyboxLf
                Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxRt = sky.SkyboxRt
                Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxUp = sky.SkyboxUp
            end
            sky.SkyboxBk = ""
            sky.SkyboxDn = ""
            sky.SkyboxFt = ""
            sky.SkyboxLf = ""
            sky.SkyboxRt = ""
            sky.SkyboxUp = ""
        end
    elseif Effects.SkyBackup then
        local sky = Lighting:FindFirstChildOfClass("Sky")
        if sky then
            sky.SkyboxBk = Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxBk
            sky.SkyboxDn = Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxDn
            sky.SkyboxFt = Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxFt
            sky.SkyboxLf = Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxLf
            sky.SkyboxRt = Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxRt
            sky.SkyboxUp = Effects.SkyBackup.SkyboxUp
        end
        Effects.SkyBackup = nil
    end
end

function Effects.ApplyFullbright()
    if Config.Fullbright then
        Lighting.Brightness = Config.FullbrightValue
        Lighting.OutdoorAmbient = Color3.fromRGB(200, 200, 200)
        Lighting.Ambient = Color3.fromRGB(190, 190, 190)
    else
        Lighting.Brightness = Backup.Brightness or 2
        Lighting.OutdoorAmbient = Backup.OutdoorAmbient or Color3.fromRGB(128, 128, 128)
        Lighting.Ambient = Backup.Ambient or Color3.fromRGB(0, 0, 0)
    end
end

function Effects.ApplyTimeOfDay()
    if Config.TimeOfDayEnabled then
        Lighting.ClockTime = Config.TimeOfDay
    else
        Lighting.ClockTime = Backup.ClockTime or 14
    end
end

function Effects.ApplyAll()
    Effects.ApplyTint()
    Effects.ApplyFog()
    Effects.ApplyFullbright()
    Effects.ApplyTimeOfDay()
    Effects.ApplySkybox()
end

--==================================================
-- PREDICTION
--==================================================

local Predict = {}

-- Velocity comes off the root's assembly velocity, with the legacy Velocity
-- property as the fallback: some rigs zero the assembly velocity while an
-- ability is pushing the character, and a zero here silently disables
-- prediction instead of erroring.
function Predict.Velocity(root)
    if not root then
        return Vector3.zero
    end
    local velocity = root.AssemblyLinearVelocity
    if velocity and velocity.Magnitude > 0.05 then
        return velocity
    end
    if root.Velocity and root.Velocity.Magnitude > 0.05 then
        return root.Velocity
    end
    return Vector3.zero
end

-- Raw assembly velocity is noisy: a character running in a straight line still
-- reports frame-to-frame spikes from collisions, slope changes and the
-- animation-driven root, and multiplying that noise by the lead time makes the
-- aim point dance. A short low-pass keeps the direction the target is actually
-- going. The filter resets whenever the tracked player changes, so one target's
-- motion can never bleed into the next.
Predict.Filtered = Vector3.zero
Predict.FilteredPlayer = nil

function Predict.FilteredVelocity(root, player)
    local raw = Predict.Velocity(root)
    local dt = State.FrameTime or 0
    if dt <= 0 then
        dt = 1 / 60
    end

    if player ~= nil and player ~= Predict.FilteredPlayer then
        Predict.FilteredPlayer = player
        Predict.Filtered = raw
    else
        local alpha = 1 - math.exp(-12 * dt)
        Predict.Filtered = Predict.Filtered:Lerp(raw, alpha)
    end

    return Predict.Filtered
end

function Predict.AimPoint(root, part, amount, player)
    if not part then
        return nil
    end
    local position = part.Position
    if not amount or amount <= 0 then
        return position
    end
    local velocity = Predict.FilteredVelocity(root, player)
    if velocity.Magnitude < 0.05 then
        return position
    end
    return position + velocity * amount
end

--==================================================
-- TARGET HIGHLIGHT
--==================================================

local Highlight = {}

Highlight.Object = nil
Highlight.Current = nil

function Highlight.Set(character, color)
    if not character then
        Highlight.Clear()
        return
    end

    if Highlight.Current == character and Highlight.Object then
        Highlight.Object.FillColor = color
        return
    end

    Highlight.Clear()

    local highlight = Instance.new("Highlight")
    highlight.Name = "RIVALS_Target"
    highlight.FillColor = color
    highlight.FillTransparency = 0.6
    highlight.OutlineColor = color
    highlight.OutlineTransparency = 0.1
    highlight.DepthMode = Enum.HighlightDepthMode.AlwaysOnTop
    highlight.Adornee = character
    highlight.Parent = ScreenGui

    Highlight.Object = highlight
    Highlight.Current = character
end

function Highlight.Clear()
    if Highlight.Object then
        pcall(function()
            Highlight.Object:Destroy()
        end)
    end
    Highlight.Object = nil
    Highlight.Current = nil
end

--==================================================
-- AIMBOT
--==================================================

local Aim = {}

Aim.FOVCircle = nil
Aim.Indicator = nil
Aim.LastCameraWrite = 0
Aim.ToggleHeld = false
Aim.LastPlayer = nil
Aim.Damp = 1
Aim.BadPushes = 0
Aim.LastErrMag = nil
Aim.LastErrPlayer = nil
Aim.Locked = false
Aim.AcquiredAt = 0
Aim.WasHeld = false
Aim.TargetPlayer = nil
Aim.NoisePhase = 0
Aim.NoiseX = 0
Aim.NoiseY = 0

local function GetAimPart(entry)
    if not entry then
        return nil
    end
    return PlayerUtil.GetPart(entry.Player, AimCfg("Part"))
end

-- Candidates are scored, not just filtered. A target behind you is worth
-- nothing, a target on the other side of the map is worth nothing, and the
-- scoring has to reflect that in one number.
local function ScoreTarget(entry, mode)
    local part = GetAimPart(entry)
    if not part then
        return nil, nil
    end

    local screen, onScreen = Cam.WorldToScreen(part.Position)
    if not screen then
        return nil, nil
    end

    local crosshair = Cam.CrosshairDistance(screen)
    local center = Cam.ScreenCenter()

    if mode == "Crosshair" then
        return crosshair, part
    elseif mode == "Distance" then
        return entry.Distance, part
    elseif mode == "Health" then
        return entry.Health, part
    elseif mode == "Threat" then
        -- Something close, near the crosshair, and hurt is the threat.
        local proximity = entry.Distance / math.max(AimCfg("Distance"), 1)
        local aim = crosshair / math.max(AimCfg("FOV"), 1)
        local hurt = entry.Health / math.max(entry.MaxHealth, 1)
        return proximity * 1.6 + aim * 1.0 + hurt * 0.6, part
    end

    local delta = screen - center
    return delta.Magnitude, part
end

function Aim.Select()
    local candidates = Cache.Get()
    local bestEntry, bestPart, bestScore = nil, nil, math.huge

    for _, entry in ipairs(candidates) do
        local valid = Cache.IsTargetable(entry, {
            TeamCheck = AimCfg("TeamCheck"),
            IgnoreFriends = AimCfg("IgnoreFriends"),
            IgnoreDead = AimCfg("IgnoreDead"),
            MaxDistance = AimCfg("Distance"),
        })

        if valid then
            local part = GetAimPart(entry)
            if part then
                local screen, onScreen = Cam.WorldToScreen(part.Position)

                -- AnyAngle drops the screen-space cone entirely, which is what
                -- makes the lock work on a target that is off screen or behind
                -- the camera - the case Rivals puts you in every respawn.
                local withinFOV = AimCfg("AnyAngle") or (screen and onScreen and Cam.InFOV(screen, AimCfg("FOV")))

                if screen and withinFOV then
                    local visible = true
                    if AimCfg("VisibleOnly") or AimCfg("WallCheck") then
                        visible = PlayerUtil.IsVisible(entry.Player)
                    end

                    if visible then
                        local score, scorePart
                        if onScreen or not AimCfg("AnyAngle") then
                            score, scorePart = ScoreTarget(entry, AimCfg("Mode"))
                        else
                            -- Turn cost, not screen distance: a target behind
                            -- the view projects to a mirrored pixel that lies,
                            -- so rank those by degrees off the view axis and
                            -- park them behind every visible candidate.
                            local camera = Cam.Get()
                            if camera then
                                local toTarget = part.Position - camera.CFrame.Position
                                if toTarget.Magnitude > 0.1 then
                                    local dot = Util.Clamp(camera.CFrame.LookVector:Dot(toTarget.Unit), -1, 1)
                                    score = 1000000 + math.deg(math.acos(dot))
                                    scorePart = part
                                end
                            end
                        end
                        if score and score < bestScore then
                            bestScore = score
                            bestEntry = entry
                            bestPart = scorePart
                        end
                    end
                end
            end
        end
    end

    return bestEntry, bestPart
end

Aim.LastTarget = nil

function Aim.Update()
    -- Toggle activation reads a rising edge on the physical key, so it works
    -- even when the game consumes that press (right mouse for ADS).
    local keyDown = Util.KeyHeld(Config.AimKeybind)
    if Config.AimActivation == "Toggle" then
        if keyDown and not Aim.ToggleHeld then
            State.AimToggled = not State.AimToggled
        end
        Aim.ToggleHeld = keyDown
    else
        Aim.ToggleHeld = keyDown
    end

    -- Deterministic micro-movement. Two incommensurate sine pairs instead of
    -- math.random(): per-frame random noise is a shimmer at frame rate, which
    -- reads as a broken overlay, and it cannot be reproduced when testing.
    local dt = State.FrameTime or 0
    Aim.NoisePhase = (Aim.NoisePhase or 0) + dt
    local nt = Aim.NoisePhase
    Aim.NoiseX = math.sin(nt * 1.7) * 0.6 + math.sin(nt * 4.3 + 1.1) * 0.4
    Aim.NoiseY = math.sin(nt * 2.3 + 0.7) * 0.6 + math.sin(nt * 5.1 + 2.4) * 0.4

    if not AimActive() then
        State.Target = nil
        State.TargetPart = nil
        State.AimPoint = nil
        State.AimPointAt = 0
        Aim.LastTarget = nil
        Aim.TargetPlayer = nil
        Aim.Locked = false
        Aim.WasHeld = false
        State.AimStall = 0
        Aim.Pushed = false
        Aim.LastLook = nil
        Highlight.Clear()
        return
    end

    -- Sticky keeps the current target while it is still valid, which stops
    -- the lock from flicking between two players standing a pixel apart.
    if AimCfg("Sticky") and Aim.LastTarget then
        local entry = Cache.Find(Aim.LastTarget)
        if entry and Cache.IsTargetable(entry, {
            TeamCheck = AimCfg("TeamCheck"),
            IgnoreFriends = AimCfg("IgnoreFriends"),
            IgnoreDead = AimCfg("IgnoreDead"),
            MaxDistance = AimCfg("Distance"),
        }) then
            local part = GetAimPart(entry)
            if part then
                local screen, onScreen = Cam.WorldToScreen(part.Position)
                local inCone = AimCfg("AnyAngle") or (onScreen and Cam.InFOV(screen, AimCfg("FOV") * 1.25))
                if screen and inCone then
                    State.Target = entry
                    State.TargetPart = part
                    Aim.LastTarget = entry.Player
                    return
                end
            end
        end
        Aim.LastTarget = nil
    end

    local entry, part = Aim.Select()
    State.Target = entry
    State.TargetPart = part
    Aim.LastTarget = entry and entry.Player or nil

    -- A new target is a new acquisition: reset the loop's memory so the old
    -- target's error, damping and prediction cannot leak into the new one. If
    -- the key is already down, the Human ramp and reaction delay start over
    -- too, which is what a hand does when it flicks between two players.
    local player = entry and entry.Player or nil
    if player ~= Aim.TargetPlayer then
        Aim.TargetPlayer = player
        State.AimPoint = nil
        State.AimPointAt = 0
        Aim.Locked = false
        Aim.Damp = 1
        Aim.BadPushes = 0
        Aim.LastErrMag = nil
        Aim.Pushed = false
        if Aim.WasHeld then
            Aim.AcquiredAt = os.clock()
        end
    end
end

local function ActivationHeld()
    -- Rage owns the gate entirely unless the player asked for the key back.
    if Config.RageEnabled and not Config.RageRequireKey then
        return true
    end
    local mode = Config.AimActivation
    if mode == "Always" then
        return true
    elseif mode == "Toggle" then
        return State.AimToggled
    end
    -- Hold reads the physical key state, not the InputBegan event. Rivals
    -- consumes right mouse for ADS, so the event path never fires for the most
    -- natural aim key in the game; IsMouseButtonPressed still reports it.
    if State.AimHeld then
        return true
    end
    return Util.KeyHeld(Config.AimKeybind)
end

local function SmoothStep(t)
    t = Util.Clamp(t or 0, 0, 1)
    return t * t * (3 - 2 * t)
end

-- The Smoothness slider is a 0..30 "how slow" knob. It has to become a
-- convergence rate in radians per second, not a fraction of the error applied
-- once per frame: a per-frame fraction closes four times faster at 240 Hz than
-- at 60 Hz, which is why the same settings felt crisp on one machine and
-- syrupy on another. One conversion through a 60 Hz reference makes the value
-- behave identically at any refresh rate.
function Aim.BaseRate()
    local smooth = AimCfg("Smoothness") or 0
    if smooth <= 0 then
        return math.huge
    end

    local alpha60 = 1 / (1 + smooth * 0.35)
    alpha60 = Util.Clamp(alpha60 * (AimCfg("Sensitivity") or 1), 0.02, 1)
    if alpha60 >= 0.999 then
        return math.huge
    end
    return -math.log(1 - alpha60) * 60
end

-- Glide trades speed for smoothness the way a hand does: a long flick is fast,
-- the last degree is slow. The scale multiplies the convergence rate, so it
-- changes the shape of the approach without changing where the lock ends up.
function Aim.CurveScale(errRad)
    local curve = AimCfg("Curve") or "Linear"
    if curve == "Linear" then
        return 1
    end

    local far = Util.Clamp(math.deg(errRad) / 6, 0, 1)
    local near = 0.55
    local gain = near + (1.45 - near) * SmoothStep(far)
    if curve == "Human" then
        -- A hand undershoots its first correction and catches up over the next
        -- few frames. 0.75 keeps that readable without feeling asleep.
        gain = gain * 0.75
    end
    return gain
end

-- The Human curve also ramps the gain in over the first ~140 ms after the lock
-- is acquired. Jumping straight to full gain on a fresh target is the single
-- most robotic-looking part of a normal aimbot.
function Aim.HumanScale(now)
    if (AimCfg("Curve") or "") ~= "Human" then
        return 1
    end
    local t = (now - (Aim.AcquiredAt or now)) / 0.14
    return 0.2 + 0.8 * SmoothStep(t)
end

-- Optional reaction delay. Zero starts the lock on the same frame the key goes
-- down; anything above that is a deliberate human pause.
function Aim.ReactionReady(now)
    local reaction = AimCfg("Reaction") or 0
    if reaction <= 0 then
        return true
    end
    return (now - (Aim.AcquiredAt or 0)) >= reaction
end

-- Blend the rotation part of two CFrames by `alpha` along the shortest arc.
-- CFrame:Lerp blends positions too, which is wrong here: the camera stays at
-- its origin and only its facing changes. Axis-angle also advances by a
-- constant angle for the step, which is exactly what a glide should look like.
local function StepRotation(current, desired, alpha)
    if alpha >= 0.999 then
        return desired
    end
    if alpha <= 0.001 then
        return current
    end

    local delta = current:Inverse() * desired
    local axis, angle = delta:ToAxisAngle()
    if angle < 1e-5 then
        return current
    end
    return current * CFrame.fromAxisAngle(axis, angle * alpha)
end

-- Pointer steering in angles, with a signed calibration instead of a sign
-- flag. A pixel-proportional loop has to guess how many counts map to a degree
-- of view rotation, and that ratio changes with in-game sensitivity, FOV and
-- DPI. A magnitude-only calibration plus a "flip the sign if it went the wrong
-- way" heuristic oscillates: one noisy frame inverts the polarity, the next
-- frame inverts it back, and the view saws left-right around the target while
-- the error climbs. This keeps the response as one signed number per axis -
-- radians of view rotation per mouse count - measured directly from what the
-- previous push did. Polarity is a property of that number, not a separate
-- state that can disagree with it.
--
-- Seed values come from a live probe on this game: 300 counts of +X produced
-- +10.36 degrees of yaw under atan2(look.X, -look.Z), and +Y counts pitch the
-- view down. The probe only sets the first step; every frame after it
-- re-measures.
Aim.Kx = 0.000603            -- radians of yaw per count, signed
Aim.Ky = -0.000603           -- radians of pitch per count, signed
Aim.K_MIN = 1e-5             -- 100000 counts/rad
Aim.K_MAX = 0.05             -- 20 counts/rad
Aim.Alpha = 0.5              -- calibration blend, old vs measured

local function ViewAngles(camera)
    local look = camera.CFrame.LookVector
    return math.atan2(look.X, -look.Z), math.asin(Util.Clamp(look.Y, -1, 1))
end

local function WrapAngle(angle)
    if angle > math.pi then
        return angle - 2 * math.pi
    elseif angle < -math.pi then
        return angle + 2 * math.pi
    end
    return angle
end

-- Folds a raw measurement into the stored response. A sample only counts when
-- the push was big enough to produce a readable rotation and the result is
-- physically plausible, so a frame where the game clamped the delta (or the
-- camera was rewritten by the match logic) cannot poison the estimate.
local function BlendMagnitude(old, measured)
    if measured ~= measured then
        return old
    end
    local magnitude = math.abs(measured)
    if magnitude < Aim.K_MIN or magnitude > Aim.K_MAX then
        return old
    end
    -- A single frame is not evidence. The game clamps large deltas, its own
    -- camera code recentres on some actions, and a frame that lands during
    -- either produces a ratio nowhere near the real one. Anything more than a
    -- factor of four away from the running estimate is a bad sample, not a new
    -- sensitivity - accepting it sends the loop to the step ceiling and the
    -- view parks a screen away.
    local current = math.abs(old)
    if current > 0 and (magnitude > current * 4 or magnitude < current * 0.25) then
        return old
    end
    local blended = current * (1 - Aim.Alpha) + magnitude * Aim.Alpha
    return old < 0 and -blended or blended
end

function Aim.Step(aimPoint)
    local camera = Cam.Get()
    if not camera then
        return false
    end

    local yawNow, pitchNow = ViewAngles(camera)
    local look = camera.CFrame.LookVector

    -- Calibrate the response from the previous push. Magnitude only: the
    -- polarity of an axis is a property of the game and the OS, not of any one
    -- frame, and letting a single measurement carry a sign in makes the loop
    -- invert itself the first time an unrelated camera event lands in the same
    -- frame. A wrong polarity is corrected once, below, after a run of frames
    -- that all got worse - twelve in a row, which no noise produces.
    if Aim.Pushed and Aim.LastYaw then
        local dYaw = WrapAngle(yawNow - Aim.LastYaw)
        local dPitch = pitchNow - (Aim.LastPitch or pitchNow)
        local pushedX = Aim.PushedX or 0
        local pushedY = Aim.PushedY or 0

        if math.abs(pushedX) >= 1 then
            Aim.Kx = BlendMagnitude(Aim.Kx, dYaw / pushedX)
        end

        if math.abs(pushedY) >= 1 then
            Aim.Ky = BlendMagnitude(Aim.Ky, dPitch / pushedY)
        end

        -- Did the view move at all? That, not the error, is what separates a
        -- dead pointer from a target that is strafing.
        local moved = math.deg(math.acos(Util.Clamp(Aim.LastLook:Dot(look), -1, 1)))
        if moved < 0.008 then
            State.AimStall = (State.AimStall or 0) + 1
        else
            State.AimStall = 0
        end
    end

    Aim.LastLook = look
    Aim.LastYaw = yawNow
    Aim.LastPitch = pitchNow

    if (State.AimStall or 0) >= 5 and Config.AimMethod ~= "Mouse" then
        State.AimDelivery = "camera"
        State.AimRetryAt = os.clock() + 2
        State.AimStatus = "pointer dead -> camera"
        Aim.Pushed = false
        return false
    end

    local localPoint = camera.CFrame:PointToObjectSpace(aimPoint)
    if localPoint.Magnitude < 0.2 then
        State.AimStatus = "on target"
        Aim.Pushed = false
        return true
    end

    -- Slack before the loop declares a lock, in degrees of view angle. Tighter
    -- than a quarter degree just burns counts fighting the game's own camera
    -- smoothing; at a 30 stud engagement a quarter degree is under a pixel.
    local deadR = math.rad(AimCfg("Deadzone"))
    local horizontal = math.sqrt(localPoint.X * localPoint.X + localPoint.Z * localPoint.Z)
    local yawError = math.atan2(localPoint.X, -localPoint.Z)
    local pitchError = math.atan2(localPoint.Y, horizontal)

    -- Hysteresis: enter at the deadzone, leave a little outside it. Without
    -- this the last fraction of a degree flickers between "locked" and one more
    -- correction while a target strafes, which the eye reads as jitter.
    local releaseR = Aim.Locked and deadR * 1.8 or deadR
    if math.abs(yawError) <= releaseR and math.abs(pitchError) <= releaseR then
        Aim.Locked = true
        Aim.Pushed = false
        Aim.LastErrMag = nil
        State.AimStatus = "locked"
        return true
    end
    Aim.Locked = false

    -- Rivals applies mouse acceleration, so the counts-per-degree measured at a
    -- large delta is not the ratio at a small one. A linear model on top of that
    -- converges and then rings: the error drops to single digits, then climbs
    -- back through 30, 80, 200 and returns. Damping is the term that makes the
    -- loop stable when the model is wrong - shrink the step while the last one
    -- made the error worse, grow it back while the error is falling.
    local targetPlayer = State.Target and State.Target.Player
    local errMag = math.sqrt(yawError * yawError + pitchError * pitchError)
    if Aim.LastErrMag and Aim.LastErrPlayer == targetPlayer then
        if errMag > Aim.LastErrMag * 1.15 and errMag > math.rad(1.5) then
            -- Only a real miss damps. Below a degree and a half the error is
            -- sub-count noise, and shrinking the gain there just makes the
            -- last few pixels take longer than the first hundred.
            Aim.Damp = math.max(0.25, Aim.Damp * 0.5)
            Aim.BadPushes = (Aim.BadPushes or 0) + 1
        elseif errMag < Aim.LastErrMag * 0.9 then
            Aim.Damp = math.min(1, Aim.Damp * 1.4)
            Aim.BadPushes = 0
        end
    else
        Aim.Damp = 1
        Aim.BadPushes = 0
    end

    -- Twelve frames in a row where the view went the wrong way is not noise:
    -- it means both axes are inverted for this game. One correction, then the
    -- counter starts over, so this can never become an oscillation.
    if (Aim.BadPushes or 0) >= 12 then
        Aim.Kx = -Aim.Kx
        Aim.Ky = -Aim.Ky
        Aim.BadPushes = 0
        Aim.Damp = 1
    end
    Aim.LastErrMag = errMag
    Aim.LastErrPlayer = targetPlayer
    _G.RIVALS_AIMDAMP = Aim.Damp

    -- Human micro-movement, faded in only once the lock is clearly outside the
    -- deadzone. Adding it at the boundary would make the loop fight itself
    -- every frame and never settle.
    if (AimCfg("Shake") or 0) > 0 then
        local amp = math.rad(AimCfg("Shake") * 0.05)
        local fade = Util.Clamp((errMag - deadR) / math.rad(1.5), 0, 1)
        yawError = yawError + (Aim.NoiseX or 0) * amp * fade
        pitchError = pitchError + (Aim.NoiseY or 0) * amp * fade
    end

    -- Divide the angle by the response to get the counts. No sign multiply:
    -- if the stored response is negative, the push comes out negative, which is
    -- exactly what an inverted axis needs.
    --
    -- Rate, not per-frame fraction: a 240 Hz client must not close the angle
    -- four times faster than a 60 Hz one. The curve shapes that rate by
    -- distance, and Damp / Human add the stability and the acquisition ramp.
    local dt = State.FrameTime or 0
    if dt <= 0 then
        dt = 1 / 60
    end

    local rate = Aim.BaseRate() * Aim.Damp * Aim.CurveScale(errMag) * Aim.HumanScale(os.clock())
    local alpha
    if rate == math.huge then
        alpha = 1
    else
        alpha = 1 - math.exp(-rate * dt)
    end
    alpha = Util.Clamp(alpha, 0, 1)

    local pushX = (yawError / Aim.Kx) * alpha
    local pushY = (pitchError / Aim.Ky) * alpha

    local magnitude = math.sqrt(pushX * pushX + pushY * pushY)
    local maxStep = AimCfg("MaxStep") * Util.Clamp(dt * 60, 0.25, 3)
    if magnitude > maxStep then
        local scale = maxStep / magnitude
        pushX = pushX * scale
        pushY = pushY * scale
        magnitude = maxStep
    end

    if magnitude < 0.5 then
        -- Below one count the pointer cannot express the step; treat it as on
        -- target instead of stalling forever on a sub-count error.
        Aim.Pushed = false
        State.AimStatus = "locked"
        return true
    end

    Aim.PushedX = pushX
    Aim.PushedY = pushY

    if not Util.MoveMouse(pushX, pushY) then
        State.AimStatus = "no mousemoverel"
        State.AimDelivery = "camera"
        State.AimRetryAt = os.clock() + 2
        Aim.Pushed = false
        return false
    end

    Aim.Pushed = true
    -- Counts per degree of yaw is the number a user can compare against their
    -- in-game sensitivity, so that is what the status line shows.
    State.AimGain = (1 / Aim.Kx) * math.pi / 180
    State.AimStatus = string.format("tracking %.0f/%.0f", math.deg(yawError), math.deg(pitchError))
    return true
end

-- The menu owns the cursor while it is open, so steering the pointer then
-- would drag the system cursor across the UI instead of the view. A game that
-- is not the foreground window swallows the same deltas, so both blocks are
-- reported by name - "aimlock does nothing" is usually one of these two.
function Aim.SteerBlock()
    if Window and Window.Open and not Window.Minimized then
        return "menu open"
    end
    if not Util.Active() then
        return "game not focused"
    end
    return nil
end

function Aim.CanSteer()
    return Aim.SteerBlock() == nil
end

-- Shared by both methods: the offset lives in camera space, so +X is right of
-- the view and +Y is up regardless of where the target is in the world.
local function OffsetPoint(aimPoint, camera)
    if Config.AimOffsetX == 0 and Config.AimOffsetY == 0 then
        return aimPoint
    end
    local rotation = camera.CFrame - camera.CFrame.Position
    return aimPoint
        + rotation.RightVector * Config.AimOffsetX
        + rotation.UpVector * Config.AimOffsetY
end

-- One aim point per frame, shared by the pointer and camera paths. Prediction
-- is filtered and offset once; recomputing it in both paths would double the
-- smoothing and make the lead depend on input method.
function Aim.GetPoint()
    if State.AimPoint and State.AimPointFrame == State.FrameCount then
        return State.AimPoint
    end

    local target = State.Target
    local part = State.TargetPart
    if not target or not part or not target.Alive then
        return nil
    end

    local camera = Cam.Get()
    if not camera then
        return nil
    end

    local point = Predict.AimPoint(target.Root, part, AimCfg("Prediction"), target.Player)
    if not point then
        return nil
    end

    point = OffsetPoint(point, camera)
    State.AimPoint = point
    State.AimPointAt = os.clock()
    State.AimPointFrame = State.FrameCount
    return point
end

-- Which path is allowed to move the view this frame. "camera" is sticky once
-- the pointer has proven dead, and Auto retries the pointer every couple of
-- seconds so walking from the lobby into a match upgrades delivery by itself.
function Aim.CameraActive()
    if not AimActive() then
        return false
    end
    if State.AimDelivery == "camera" then
        return true
    end
    return Config.AimMethod == "Camera"
end

function Aim.Apply()
    if not AimActive() then
        State.AimStatus = "off"
        return
    end
    if not ActivationHeld() then
        Aim.WasHeld = false
        Aim.Locked = false
        State.AimStatus = "key up"
        return
    end

    -- Acquisition edge: reaction and the Human ramp are timed from the frame
    -- the key goes down, not from whenever a target happens to be selected.
    local now = os.clock()
    if not Aim.WasHeld then
        Aim.WasHeld = true
        Aim.AcquiredAt = now
        Aim.Locked = false
        Aim.Damp = 1
        Aim.BadPushes = 0
        Aim.LastErrMag = nil
        Aim.Pushed = false
    end

    local target = State.Target
    if not target or not State.TargetPart or not target.Alive then
        State.AimStatus = "no target"
        return
    end

    if AimCfg("TeamCheck") and target.Teammate then
        State.AimStatus = "teammate"
        return
    end

    if not Aim.ReactionReady(now) then
        State.AimStatus = "reacting"
        return
    end

    local camera = Cam.Get()
    if not camera then
        return
    end

    local aimPoint = Aim.GetPoint()
    if not aimPoint then
        return
    end

    -- Delivery. Rivals only applies synthetic pointer deltas while it is
    -- holding the cursor (MouseBehavior LockCenter / LockCurrentPosition); with
    -- a free cursor the same counts move the OS pointer and nothing else, which
    -- is why pointer aim looks dead in the lobby. Auto therefore keys off the
    -- capture state and falls back to the CFrame write, which works either way.
    local method = Config.AimMethod
    if method == "Camera" then
        State.AimDelivery = "camera"
    elseif method == "Mouse" then
        if not Util.PointerCaptured() then
            State.AimStatus = "cursor free - click the game"
            return
        end
        State.AimDelivery = "mouse"
    else
        if not Util.PointerCaptured() then
            State.AimDelivery = "camera"
            State.AimStall = 0
        elseif State.AimDelivery == "camera" and os.clock() >= (State.AimRetryAt or 0) then
            State.AimDelivery = "mouse"
            State.AimStall = 0
            Aim.Pushed = false
            Aim.LastLook = nil
        end
    end

    local wantMouse = State.AimDelivery ~= "camera"

    if wantMouse then
        local block = Aim.SteerBlock()
        if block then
            State.AimStatus = block
            return
        end
        local ok = Aim.Step(aimPoint)
        if not ok and State.AimDelivery == "camera" then
            -- Handoff happens in the same frame the pointer is declared dead,
            -- so the lock never drops a frame mid-fight.
            Aim.SteerCamera()
        end
    else
        State.AimStatus = State.AimDelivery == "camera" and "camera (auto)" or "camera"
    end

    -- Auto fire is downstream of the lock, not part of it: by the time the
    -- click leaves, the view is already on the target for this frame. A
    -- configured reaction delay holds the trigger too, otherwise the first
    -- shot leaves before the hand has finished acquiring.
    if AimCfg("AutoFire") and Aim.ReactionReady(os.clock()) then
        if os.clock() - (State.LastFire or 0) >= AimCfg("AutoFireDelay") then
            if Aim.CanSteer() then
                State.LastFire = os.clock()
                Util.Click()
            end
        end
    end
end

-- The CFrame path, method-agnostic so it can be the fallback as well as the
-- chosen method. Called last in the frame (see the RenderStep binding in the
-- bootstrap) because a game that owns its camera rewrites it earlier.
function Aim.SteerCamera()
    if not AimActive() then
        return false
    end
    if not ActivationHeld() then
        return false
    end

    local target = State.Target
    if not target or not State.TargetPart or not target.Alive then
        return false
    end

    if AimCfg("TeamCheck") and target.Teammate then
        return false
    end

    if not Aim.ReactionReady(os.clock()) then
        State.AimStatus = "reacting"
        return false
    end

    local camera = Cam.Get()
    if not camera then
        return false
    end

    -- Did the previous write survive the frame? A game that recomputes the
    -- camera from its own yaw/pitch every frame silently discards ours, and
    -- "aimlock does nothing" is exactly what that looks like from outside.
    if Aim.WroteLook and Aim.WroteAt and (os.clock() - Aim.WroteAt) < 0.5 then
        local kept = math.deg(math.acos(Util.Clamp(Aim.WroteLook:Dot(camera.CFrame.LookVector), -1, 1)))
        if kept > 5 then
            State.AimLostFrames = (State.AimLostFrames or 0) + 1
        else
            State.AimLostFrames = 0
        end
    end

    local aimPoint = Aim.GetPoint()
    if not aimPoint then
        return false
    end

    local origin = camera.CFrame.Position
    local offset = aimPoint - origin
    if offset.Magnitude < 0.1 then
        return false
    end

    local localPoint = camera.CFrame:PointToObjectSpace(aimPoint)
    local horizontal = math.sqrt(localPoint.X * localPoint.X + localPoint.Z * localPoint.Z)
    local yawError = math.atan2(localPoint.X, -localPoint.Z)
    local pitchError = math.atan2(localPoint.Y, horizontal)
    local errMag = math.sqrt(yawError * yawError + pitchError * pitchError)

    -- Same deadzone and hysteresis as the pointer path, so the camera does not
    -- keep writing sub-pixel corrections forever once the target is centred.
    local deadR = math.rad(AimCfg("Deadzone"))
    local releaseR = Aim.Locked and deadR * 1.8 or deadR
    if math.abs(yawError) <= releaseR and math.abs(pitchError) <= releaseR then
        Aim.Locked = true
        State.AimStatus = "locked"
        return false
    end
    Aim.Locked = false

    -- Same curve as the pointer path, so changing Input Method does not change
    -- how the lock feels. The camera is written directly, so it skips the
    -- pointer loop's calibration damping, which exists to absorb count errors
    -- the camera path never has.
    local dt = State.FrameTime or 0
    if dt <= 0 then
        dt = 1 / 60
    end

    local rate = Aim.BaseRate() * Aim.CurveScale(errMag) * Aim.HumanScale(os.clock())
    local alpha
    if rate == math.huge then
        alpha = 1
    else
        alpha = 1 - math.exp(-rate * dt)
    end
    alpha = Util.Clamp(alpha, 0, 1)

    if (AimCfg("Shake") or 0) > 0 then
        local amp = math.rad(AimCfg("Shake") * 0.05)
        local fade = Util.Clamp((errMag - deadR) / math.rad(1.5), 0, 1)
        local distance = offset.Magnitude
        offset = offset
            + camera.CFrame.RightVector * ((Aim.NoiseX or 0) * amp * fade * distance)
            + camera.CFrame.UpVector * ((Aim.NoiseY or 0) * amp * fade * distance)
    end

    local currentRotation = camera.CFrame - camera.CFrame.Position
    local desiredRotation = CFrame.lookAt(Vector3.zero, offset)
    local smoothed = CFrame.new(origin) * StepRotation(currentRotation, desiredRotation, alpha)

    camera.CFrame = smoothed
    Aim.LastCameraWrite = os.clock()
    Aim.WroteLook = smoothed.LookVector
    Aim.WroteAt = os.clock()

    if (State.AimLostFrames or 0) >= 8 then
        State.AimStatus = "camera overwritten"
    else
        State.AimStatus = "camera write"
    end
    return true
end

function Aim.CameraApply()
    if Destroyed then
        return
    end
    if not Aim.CameraActive() then
        return
    end
    Aim.SteerCamera()
end

--==================================================
-- AIM VISUALS
--==================================================

local AimVisual = {}

function AimVisual.Init()
    if not Draw.Available then
        return
    end

    Aim.FOVCircle = Draw.New("Circle")
    if Aim.FOVCircle then
        Aim.FOVCircle.Thickness = Config.FOVThickness
        Aim.FOVCircle.Filled = Config.FOVFilled
        Aim.FOVCircle.Color = Config.FOVColor
        Aim.FOVCircle.Transparency = 0.85
        Aim.FOVCircle.NumSides = 64
    end

    Aim.Indicator = Draw.New("Circle")
    if Aim.Indicator then
        Aim.Indicator.Thickness = 2
        Aim.Indicator.Filled = false
        Aim.Indicator.Color = Config.TargetIndicatorColor
        Aim.Indicator.Transparency = 0.9
        Aim.Indicator.NumSides = 24
    end

    -- Aimlock Sense: a box, a lead line and a telemetry label on whatever the
    -- aim loop is actually holding. The FOV circle shows where a target can be
    -- found; this shows what the lock is doing with the one it picked.
    -- A Quad is used instead of a Square because its corners are explicit, so
    -- the box stays centred on the target no matter how the executor anchors
    -- Square.Position.
    Aim.SenseBox = Draw.New("Quad")
    if Aim.SenseBox then
        Aim.SenseBox.Filled = false
        Aim.SenseBox.Thickness = 1
        Aim.SenseBox.Color = Config.AimSenseColor
    end

    Aim.SenseLine = Draw.New("Line")
    if Aim.SenseLine then
        Aim.SenseLine.Thickness = 1
        Aim.SenseLine.Color = Config.AimSenseColor
        Aim.SenseLine.Transparency = 0.45
    end

    Aim.SenseText = Draw.New("Text")
    if Aim.SenseText then
        Aim.SenseText.Center = true
        Aim.SenseText.Outline = true
        Aim.SenseText.Font = 2
        Aim.SenseText.Color = Config.AimSenseColor
    end
end

function AimVisual.Update()
    if not Draw.Available then
        return
    end

    local center = Cam.ScreenCenter()

    if Aim.FOVCircle then
        local show = Config.ShowFOV and AimActive()
        Aim.FOVCircle.Visible = show
        if show then
            Aim.FOVCircle.Position = center
            Aim.FOVCircle.Radius = AimCfg("FOV")
            Aim.FOVCircle.Thickness = Config.FOVThickness
            Aim.FOVCircle.Filled = Config.FOVFilled
            Aim.FOVCircle.Color = Config.FOVColor
            Aim.FOVCircle.Transparency = Config.FOVFilled
                and (1 - Config.FOVFillTransparency) or 0.85
        end
    end

    if Aim.Indicator then
        local part = State.TargetPart
        local show = Config.TargetIndicator and AimActive() and part ~= nil
        Aim.Indicator.Visible = show
        if show then
            local screen, onScreen = Cam.WorldToScreen(part.Position)
            if screen and onScreen then
                Aim.Indicator.Position = screen
                Aim.Indicator.Radius = 26
                Aim.Indicator.Color = Config.TargetIndicatorColor
                Aim.Indicator.Visible = true
            else
                Aim.Indicator.Visible = false
            end
        end
    end

    local target = State.Target
    local part = State.TargetPart
    local sense = Config.AimSense and AimActive() and target ~= nil and part ~= nil
    local screen, onScreen, depth = nil, false, 0

    if sense then
        screen, onScreen, depth = Cam.WorldToScreen(part.Position)
    end

    local senseVisible = sense and screen ~= nil and onScreen and depth > 0
    local senseHeight = 0

    if Aim.SenseBox then
        Aim.SenseBox.Visible = senseVisible
        if senseVisible then
            local height = Util.Clamp(2600 / math.max(depth, 1), 24, 180)
            local width = height * 0.62
            senseHeight = height
            local pulse = 0.5 + 0.3 * (0.5 + 0.5 * math.sin(os.clock() * 5))

            Aim.SenseBox.PointA = Vector2.new(screen.X - width / 2, screen.Y - height / 2)
            Aim.SenseBox.PointB = Vector2.new(screen.X + width / 2, screen.Y - height / 2)
            Aim.SenseBox.PointC = Vector2.new(screen.X + width / 2, screen.Y + height / 2)
            Aim.SenseBox.PointD = Vector2.new(screen.X - width / 2, screen.Y + height / 2)
            Aim.SenseBox.Thickness = Config.AimSenseThickness or 1
            Aim.SenseBox.Color = Config.AimSenseColor
            Aim.SenseBox.Transparency = pulse
        end
    end

    if Aim.SenseLine then
        Aim.SenseLine.Visible = senseVisible
        if senseVisible then
            Aim.SenseLine.From = center
            Aim.SenseLine.To = screen
            Aim.SenseLine.Color = Config.AimSenseColor
            Aim.SenseLine.Transparency = 0.55
        end
    end

    if Aim.SenseText then
        Aim.SenseText.Visible = senseVisible
        if senseVisible then
            local camera = Cam.Get()
            local error = 0
            if camera then
                local delta = part.Position - camera.CFrame.Position
                if delta.Magnitude > 0.1 then
                    error = math.deg(math.acos(Util.Clamp(
                        camera.CFrame.LookVector:Dot(delta.Unit), -1, 1
                    )))
                end
            end

            Aim.SenseText.Text = string.format(
                "%s  %dm  %d/%d HP  %.1f deg",
                target.Name,
                math.floor(target.Distance / 3 + 0.5),
                math.floor(target.Health + 0.5),
                math.floor(target.MaxHealth + 0.5),
                error
            )
            Aim.SenseText.Size = Config.AimSenseTextSize
            Aim.SenseText.Position = Vector2.new(screen.X, screen.Y - senseHeight / 2 - 15)
            Aim.SenseText.Color = Config.AimSenseColor
            Aim.SenseText.Transparency = 0.05
        end
    end

    if Config.TargetHighlight and AimActive() and State.Target then
        Highlight.Set(State.Target.Character, Config.TargetHighlightColor)
    else
        Highlight.Clear()
    end
end

_G.RIVALS_AIM = Aim

--==================================================
-- TRIGGERBOT
--==================================================

local Trigger = {}

Trigger.LastFire = 0
Trigger.Held = false

local function TriggerActivation()
    if Config.TriggerMode == "Always" then
        return true
    end
    if Trigger.Held then
        return true
    end
    return Util.KeyHeld(Config.TriggerKeybind)
end

local function TriggerTarget()
    local bestEntry, bestPart = nil, nil
    local bestDistance = math.huge

    local center = Cam.ScreenCenter()

    for _, entry in ipairs(Cache.Get()) do
        if Cache.IsTargetable(entry, {
            TeamCheck = Config.TriggerTeamCheck,
            IgnoreDead = true,
            MaxDistance = Config.AimDistance,
        }) then
            local part = PlayerUtil.GetPart(entry.Player, Config.TriggerPart)
            if part then
                local screen, onScreen = Cam.WorldToScreen(part.Position)
                if screen and onScreen then
                    local distance = (screen - center).Magnitude
                    if distance <= Config.TriggerFOV and distance < bestDistance then
                        if not Config.TriggerVisibleOnly or PlayerUtil.IsVisible(entry.Player) then
                            bestDistance = distance
                            bestEntry = entry
                            bestPart = part
                        end
                    end
                end
            end
        end
    end

    return bestEntry, bestPart
end

function Trigger.Update()
    if not Config.TriggerEnabled then
        return
    end
    if not TriggerActivation() then
        return
    end
    if not Util.Active() then
        return
    end
    if Window and Window.Open and not Window.Minimized then
        return
    end

    local entry = TriggerTarget()
    if not entry then
        return
    end

    if os.clock() - Trigger.LastFire < Config.TriggerDelay then
        return
    end

    Trigger.LastFire = os.clock()
